E Conversations

Curbside is not upstairs placement

Extended conversation · 20 turns · 10 gaps

Rafael: Customer / Amara: Delivery driver

Complete the numbered gaps. Use each bank entry once, without changing its form.

Word bank
  • authorization
  • service amendment
  • Assembly
  • upgrade request
  • booked service
  • timing confirmation
  • service scope
  • change acceptance
  • scope mismatch
  • quotation
  1. Rafael
    I need it upstairs in my apartment. When I booked delivery, I thought that meant bringing it to the room where I'll use it.
  2. Amara
    I understand the expectation. The 1 here is curbside delivery. This booking does not include inside service or upstairs placement.
  3. Rafael
    That's a problem. Can the upstairs part be added now? I wasn't planning for the item to stop outside.
  4. Amara
    I cannot make that 2 myself. Dispatch can review an upgrade or rearrangement, but I do not have an approved change to carry out.
  5. Rafael
    Do you mean the carrier never offers upstairs delivery, or just that it isn't part of the service I've booked?
  6. Amara
    I am explaining the 3 of this booking. Whether another service can be offered needs checking, so I should not give you a definite yes or no yet.
  7. Rafael
    Would it cost extra? Please don't take my asking as agreement to whatever the price turns out to be.
  8. Amara
    No 4 has been provided. You have not accepted an unknown price simply by asking what alternatives might be available.
  9. Rafael
    And could it happen today? I need to check both the price and the date before deciding.
  10. Amara
    I have no 5 either. Availability, price, and timing all need a checked response before you can assess a specific proposal.
  11. Rafael
    Please ask dispatch. Make the request specific: upstairs placement, not just customer unhappy with delivery.
  12. Amara
    I can send an 6 for upstairs placement. I will explain that curbside is booked and that you want them to review a different arrangement.
  13. Rafael
    I don't need assembly or installation considered at this point. First I need to know whether the item can be placed upstairs.
  14. Amara
    Understood. 7 is separate from placement, and I will not expand your request into installation or other work you have not asked about.
  15. Rafael
    I want it handled through the booking, not an informal arrangement. I need a clear record of what's included.
  16. Amara
    That requires the proper 8 and a confirmed arrangement. I cannot agree to an informal extra charge or treat that as a replacement for the process.
  17. Rafael
    So I'll get the actual proposal before deciding? There isn't a confirmed extra service or charge for me to accept yet.
  18. Amara
    Yes. Your 9 would concern a specific proposed service, price, and timing. At present those details remain unknown.
  19. Rafael
    Please pass that on. We've discussed what I wanted, but nothing here says the upgrade or unloading has happened.
  20. Amara
    I will. The 10 is clear, and I will refer it to dispatch. This conversation does not confirm an upgrade or any completed unloading.

Answers and explanations
  1. booked service Turn 2. Booked service is the recorded curbside arrangement, which differs from the requested upstairs placement.
  2. service amendment Turn 4. Service amendment would change the authorized work; the driver cannot approve it in this conversation.
  3. service scope Turn 6. Service scope identifies what this booking includes; it does not establish every possible service the carrier offers.
  4. quotation Turn 8. Quotation would specify a checked price; none exists, and a question does not authorize an unknown charge.
  5. timing confirmation Turn 10. Timing confirmation is absent, so today or any replacement date cannot be promised.
  6. upgrade request Turn 12. Upgrade request names the proposed additional service for review without representing it as approved.
  7. Assembly Turn 14. Assembly means putting parts together and is separate from the placement request Rafael wants reviewed.
  8. authorization Turn 16. Authorization must cover the specific changed service; an informal understanding with the driver is not supplied here.
  9. change acceptance Turn 18. Change acceptance applies to a specific confirmed proposal; none has yet been offered or accepted.
  10. scope mismatch Turn 20. Scope mismatch is the difference between booked curbside delivery and requested upstairs placement, still awaiting review.
Read the complete conversation
  1. Rafael: I need it upstairs in my apartment. When I booked delivery, I thought that meant bringing it to the room where I'll use it.
  2. Amara: I understand the expectation. The booked service here is curbside delivery. This booking does not include inside service or upstairs placement.
  3. Rafael: That's a problem. Can the upstairs part be added now? I wasn't planning for the item to stop outside.
  4. Amara: I cannot make that service amendment myself. Dispatch can review an upgrade or rearrangement, but I do not have an approved change to carry out.
  5. Rafael: Do you mean the carrier never offers upstairs delivery, or just that it isn't part of the service I've booked?
  6. Amara: I am explaining the service scope of this booking. Whether another service can be offered needs checking, so I should not give you a definite yes or no yet.
  7. Rafael: Would it cost extra? Please don't take my asking as agreement to whatever the price turns out to be.
  8. Amara: No quotation has been provided. You have not accepted an unknown price simply by asking what alternatives might be available.
  9. Rafael: And could it happen today? I need to check both the price and the date before deciding.
  10. Amara: I have no timing confirmation either. Availability, price, and timing all need a checked response before you can assess a specific proposal.
  11. Rafael: Please ask dispatch. Make the request specific: upstairs placement, not just customer unhappy with delivery.
  12. Amara: I can send an upgrade request for upstairs placement. I will explain that curbside is booked and that you want them to review a different arrangement.
  13. Rafael: I don't need assembly or installation considered at this point. First I need to know whether the item can be placed upstairs.
  14. Amara: Understood. Assembly is separate from placement, and I will not expand your request into installation or other work you have not asked about.
  15. Rafael: I want it handled through the booking, not an informal arrangement. I need a clear record of what's included.
  16. Amara: That requires the proper authorization and a confirmed arrangement. I cannot agree to an informal extra charge or treat that as a replacement for the process.
  17. Rafael: So I'll get the actual proposal before deciding? There isn't a confirmed extra service or charge for me to accept yet.
  18. Amara: Yes. Your change acceptance would concern a specific proposed service, price, and timing. At present those details remain unknown.
  19. Rafael: Please pass that on. We've discussed what I wanted, but nothing here says the upgrade or unloading has happened.
  20. Amara: I will. The scope mismatch is clear, and I will refer it to dispatch. This conversation does not confirm an upgrade or any completed unloading.
Dialogue in the book, page 55 ↗
Three more conversations at work

Each short conversation has its own fictional situation and facts.

The upstairs request

A driver and customer compare curbside service with the customer's expectation.

  1. Customer: I need these cartons upstairs. The order page said delivery, so I assumed inside.
  2. Driver: Your booked service level is curbside delivery; upstairs placement isn't included in that service.
  3. Customer: Is that something you can add now if I pay the difference directly?
  4. Driver: I can't add it here. Dispatch can review an inside-delivery request through the booking team.
  5. Customer: Will that mean another day, or can somebody come this afternoon instead?
  6. Driver: I don't have confirmed availability or a price. Those details need checking before you decide.
  7. Customer: Please ask about the upstairs option; curbside isn't what I thought I had arranged.
  8. Driver: I'll explain that mismatch and request a review rather than promise an immediate upgrade.
  9. Customer: Also tell them the destination is the second floor, so the request is clear.
  10. Driver: I will. Your requested location and the current service level will both be stated.
Receiving moved behind a restricted entrance

A parked driver speaks with a site contact about a new access condition.

  1. Driver: The delivery note names the loading entrance, but today's notice shows an access restriction.
  2. Site contact: That entrance is unavailable today. Could you use the visitor entrance for this shipment?
  3. Driver: I need dispatch and your receiving team to review a suitable handover point first.
  4. Site contact: I can connect you with receiving; I only coordinate visitor appointments at this building.
  5. Driver: That would help. I shouldn't treat your visitor instructions as a goods-delivery approval.
  6. Site contact: Agreed. The receiving contact is Morgan, and their desk is staffed until four.
  7. Driver: I'll explain the restricted entrance and ask Morgan which delivery arrangement can be confirmed.
  8. Site contact: Please mention that the original loading entrance is unavailable today, not permanently closed.
  9. Driver: I'll keep the timing specific and leave the alternative location unconfirmed until that discussion.
  10. Site contact: Good. I'll connect Morgan now so the people responsible can resolve the delivery arrangement.
A lobby is not every interior room

A driver clarifies the booked handover location at an office.

  1. Driver: The booking specifies inside delivery to the ground-floor lobby. Is that still your receiving point?
  2. Customer: We hoped you could take the cartons into the archive room along the corridor.
  3. Driver: That room isn't named in this booking. I'll need the service team to review the change.
  4. Customer: But it is still indoors. Doesn't inside delivery cover every room in the building?
  5. Driver: Not necessarily. This booking identifies the lobby specifically, so I can't expand that description myself.
  6. Customer: Please check, then. The archive staff expected the cartons to arrive at their desk.
  7. Driver: I'll explain their expectation and the location currently included, without promising the added movement.
  8. Customer: Can you also say we are asking about today's delivery, not future orders?
  9. Driver: Yes, I'll link the request to this shipment and today's agreed service details.
  10. Customer: That's helpful. We'll wait for the service team's answer before assuming the archive is included.

Curbside is not upstairs placement
  1. Contrast booked curbside delivery with requested upstairs placement in turns 2 and 12.
  2. Read price, availability, and timing as three unanswered questions.

On your own: read both roles. With a partner: swap roles after the first reading. Use the completed conversation to check any line you need to repeat.

Answer and explanation

D. curbside

2. Customer: "I am requesting ___ placement."

Answer and explanation

C. room-of-choice

Room-of-choice identifies the specific additional service the customer wants reviewed.

3. Driver: "The proposed change needs ___ review."

Answer and explanation

D. dispatch

Dispatch is the team that can assess the requested change here.

4. Customer: "The price and timing remain ___."

Answer and explanation

D. unknown

No checked proposal supplies either cost or a revised schedule.

Useful expressions (preserve the supplied facts): I understand you expected the item upstairs. / The booking specifies curbside delivery. / Inside service is not included in this booking. / I cannot add upstairs placement myself. / Let us clarify the difference between the booking and the request. / Dispatch can review an upgrade or rearrangement. / I do not yet know whether that service can be offered. / No upgrade price has been confirmed. / No revised delivery time is confirmed. / Would you like me to refer the upstairs-placement request? / Asking for review does not approve the service change. / I cannot agree to an informal extra charge. / You have not accepted an unknown price. / Placement, assembly, and installation are different services. / We are discussing scope, not confirming unloading. / I will send the request to dispatch for a checked response.
Vocabulary: curbside delivery: Service to the agreed curbside point under the actual booking. / inside delivery: Service bringing goods inside under specified terms. / upstairs placement: Positioning goods on an upper floor. / room-of-choice service: Delivery to a specified room when that service is offered and agreed. / booked service: Service actually recorded in the confirmed booking. / service scope: Work included and excluded in the agreed arrangement. / service boundary: Limit of what the current arrangement authorizes. / upgrade request: Proposal to change to an additional or higher service level. / rearrangement: Change to delivery arrangements requiring confirmation. / availability check: Verification that a requested service can be supplied. / quotation: Stated price for specified work and conditions. / accessorial charge: Additional freight-service charge under applicable terms. / price confirmation: Verification of the actual amount for the proposed service. / timing confirmation: Verification of when an agreed service can occur. / authorization: Permission from the appropriate decision-maker for a specific action. / service amendment: Approved change to the recorded service arrangement. / scope mismatch: Difference between the booked work and the requested work. / customer expectation: What the customer believes the service will include. / access requirement: Site or access detail relevant to reviewing the requested service. / stair carry: Carrying goods on stairs as a separately defined service where offered. / assembly: Putting product parts together, distinct from delivery. / installation: Setting up a product for use under the relevant service. / unloading status: Whether unloading has occurred, separate from discussion of scope. / change acceptance: Customer agreement to a specific confirmed proposal.

07Matching a return to its collectionTwo booked cartons and one extra+

Your goal

Match goods to a return reference, isolate an unmatched item, and request clarification without silently expanding the collection.

This lesson in the book, page 58 ↗

A Read the situation

Two booked cartons and one extra

Driver Kai arrives for return collection R62, which lists two printer cartons. Customer Dana presents those two identifiable cartons and a third carton of cables without a matching collection reference. Dispatch can query the extra carton, but no authorization to add it has been received. The two printer cartons remain identifiable under R62. The conversation establishes the mismatch and next query; it does not confirm that any carton has been loaded, collected, rejected permanently, or credited to the customer.

Kai: Driver / Dana: Customer

Check the facts · 3 questions

Choose the answer supported by the briefing. Do not assume that a proposed action has already happened.

1. What does R62 list?

Answer and explanation

A. Two printer cartons

R62 identifies two printer cartons, not an unrestricted collection of related items.

2. Which item lacks a matching reference?

Answer and explanation

D. The third carton of cables

The cable carton is additional and has no matching collection reference.

3. What is established by the discussion?

Answer and explanation

B. The mismatch and a dispatch query, not completed collection

The conversation clarifies identity and follow-up without supplying a collection or credit outcome.

B Find the words

Vocabulary and collocations

R62 matches two printer cartons. Three cartons are presented, but the cable carton has no matching reference. The extra-item query should not change the original two-carton description or make the two identified cartons disappear from the record.

24 field terms and natural word combinations
return reference
Identifier connecting goods to a particular return arrangement.confirm the return reference
collection booking
Recorded arrangement for picking up specified goods.check the collection booking
return authorization
Approval for specified goods to be returned under stated conditions.verify return authorization
return merchandise authorization
Formal return identifier or process, commonly RMA.quote the return merchandise authorization
matching reference
Identifier consistent with the relevant record.locate a matching reference
unmatched item
Item not linked to the stated collection record.isolate the unmatched item
printer carton
Outer box identified as containing a printer in the return description.identify the printer carton
cable carton
Outer box presented as containing cables.distinguish the cable carton
additional carton
Box presented beyond the recorded collection quantity.query the additional carton
booked quantity
Number of units specified in the collection arrangement.preserve the booked quantity
presented quantity
Number of units offered by the customer at collection.compare the presented quantity
collection scope
Items included in the specific pickup arrangement.clarify the collection scope
reference match
Confirmed correspondence between an item and a record identifier.establish a reference match
return label
Label linking a package to return-shipping information.check the return label
serial number
Identifier assigned to a particular individual product.verify the serial number
item description
Recorded wording identifying the type of goods.compare the item description
packing description
Account of how goods are grouped or packaged.confirm the packing description
collection amendment
Approved change to the pickup arrangement.request a collection amendment
query
Request for clarification, not an approved change.raise a query
item separation
Keeping matched and unmatched items distinct in the discussion.maintain item separation
loaded status
Record that goods have been placed on the vehicle.distinguish loaded status
collected status
Record that a pickup has actually occurred.verify collected status
credit approval
Authorized financial credit, separate from return collection.distinguish credit approval
return disposition
Decision about what happens to returned goods under the actual process.await return disposition

A collection reference, a product serial number, and a return authorization may serve different purposes. Use the actual records required by the carrier and customer process. A reference match alone does not prove loading, pickup, refund, or credit completion.

C Notice the language

Separate the extra item from the valid reference

A customer may reasonably group related equipment together, while the collection record identifies a narrower set. Explain which items match and which item needs clarification. Avoid turning one unmatched carton into a claim that the entire collection is invalid.

16 useful phrases, grouped by purpose
Identify the booking
I am here for return collection R62.
State the booked count
The booking lists two printer cartons.
Confirm the matched items
These two printer cartons match R62.
Name the additional item
The third carton contains cables according to your description.
Identify the missing link
I do not have a matching reference for that carton.
Keep the sets separate
The original two are identifiable; the cable carton is the open question.
Ask for clarification
Is there a separate collection reference for the cables?
Avoid silent expansion
I cannot treat R62 as a three-carton booking without confirmation.
Refer the query
Dispatch can query the additional carton.
Keep approval pending
No collection amendment has been authorized yet.
Avoid permanent rejection
Unmatched does not mean permanently rejected.
Preserve the item description
Two printer cartons, not three printer cartons.
Distinguish status
Matching the reference does not mean the goods are already collected.
Avoid a credit promise
This check does not approve a customer credit.
Read back the difference
Two booked, three presented, with one unmatched cable carton.
Close with the exact query
The next question is whether the cable carton has an authorized collection arrangement.
6 language and precision notes
Additional
Identifies the carton beyond the original two, not a substitute for them.
Match
Connects a specific item and record rather than similar-looking goods.
Related versus included
Cables may relate to printers without belonging to the same booking.
Query versus amendment
Asking about an extra item does not alter the collection record.
Identifiable versus collected
A successful match is not a completed pickup.
Return versus credit
Moving goods back does not itself establish a financial remedy.

D Check your understanding

Choose the precise message

Choose one best answer using the case facts and the stated purpose.

1. Which read-back is accurate?

Answer and explanation

A. R62 lists two printer cartons; the cable carton is unmatched.

The statement preserves the two matches and isolates the additional cable carton.

2. Which action is available in the conversation?

Answer and explanation

C. Ask dispatch to query the extra carton.

Dispatch can seek clarification, while adding or collecting the extra carton remains unconfirmed.

3. Which statement goes too far?

Answer and explanation

D. The unmatched carton is permanently rejected.

Missing information now does not establish a permanent rejection decision.

4. Which status distinction is essential?

Answer and explanation

C. Reference matched is not the same as collection completed.

Identity verification and actual collection are different stages with different evidence.

Check explanations in the book, page 97 ↗

E Conversations

Two booked cartons and one extra

Extended conversation · 20 turns · 10 gaps

Kai: Driver / Dana: Customer

Complete the numbered gaps. Use each bank entry once, without changing its form.

Word bank
  • booked quantity
  • unmatched item
  • matching reference
  • collected status
  • credit approval
  • collection scope
  • collection booking
  • return reference
  • collection amendment
  • cable carton
  1. Kai
    I'm here for return R62. It lists two printer cartons, but there are three cartons ready here. Could we check which is which?
  2. Dana
    These two are the printers. The third is a 1. I packed the cables separately because they go with the printers.
  3. Kai
    The two printer cartons match R62. I don't have a matching collection reference for the cables.
  4. Dana
    I thought the 2 covered everything we were returning together. Does that not include this separate box?
  5. Kai
    Not on the record I've got. We need to check the extra carton rather than quietly change a two-carton collection to three.
  6. Dana
    I don't have a 3 for that one. Can you ask dispatch whether it's covered by another arrangement?
  7. Kai
    Yes. I'll give them the cable description and explain that the printers match but the extra box doesn't have a reference here.
  8. Dana
    Please keep the 4 as two printer cartons while they check. There aren't three printers.
  9. Kai
    Exactly. Two printer cartons linked to R62, plus one separate carton you've described as cables.
  10. Dana
    Does the extra box cancel the whole 5, or can the record still show which two match?
  11. Kai
    The original two are still identifiable under R62. It's the additional cable carton that needs clarification.
  12. Dana
    Good. I don't want each printer labelled an 6 because I've brought out an extra box.
  13. Kai
    I won't do that. I'll keep the matches and the extra-item query separate. Dispatch hasn't authorized adding the cables yet.
  14. Dana
    So any 7 is still pending. Asking you about the box hasn't changed the booking.
  15. Kai
    Correct. It's a query, not an approved addition. It also isn't a permanent rejection of the cable carton.
  16. Dana
    And no 8 yet. We're still checking the paperwork, not saying you've taken any of these cartons.
  17. Kai
    Right. A reference match, loading, and a completed pickup are different stages. We've only clarified the identity question here.
  18. Dana
    It isn't 9 either, then. I'll avoid telling accounts that a return check means the credit has gone through.
  19. Kai
    Yes. My report is R62, two identified printer cartons, one additional cable carton without a matching reference, query to dispatch.
  20. Dana
    That's accurate. Keep the 10 clear while you ask about the cables. Don't change the description to three printers.

Answers and explanations
  1. cable carton Turn 2. The cable carton is the additional third package, not another printer carton under the two-carton booking.
  2. return reference Turn 4. R62 is the return reference for two printer cartons; related accessories are not automatically added to it.
  3. matching reference Turn 6. No matching reference links the cable carton to this pickup; dispatch needs to clarify its arrangement.
  4. booked quantity Turn 8. The booked quantity is two printer cartons even though three cartons have been presented.
  5. collection booking Turn 10. The original booking still identifies two printer cartons; the additional question does not erase those matches.
  6. unmatched item Turn 12. Only the cable carton is unmatched; applying that label to the two identified printers would misstate the check.
  7. collection amendment Turn 14. A collection amendment is an authorized change, not the customer's question or dispatch inquiry by itself.
  8. collected status Turn 16. No pickup has been completed during this reference check, so collected status would be premature.
  9. credit approval Turn 18. A financial credit decision is separate from matching goods to a collection reference or completing a pickup.
  10. collection scope Turn 20. The recorded scope stays at the two printer cartons unless the extra item is properly confirmed and authorized.
Read the complete conversation
  1. Kai: I'm here for return R62. It lists two printer cartons, but there are three cartons ready here. Could we check which is which?
  2. Dana: These two are the printers. The third is a cable carton. I packed the cables separately because they go with the printers.
  3. Kai: The two printer cartons match R62. I don't have a matching collection reference for the cables.
  4. Dana: I thought the return reference covered everything we were returning together. Does that not include this separate box?
  5. Kai: Not on the record I've got. We need to check the extra carton rather than quietly change a two-carton collection to three.
  6. Dana: I don't have a matching reference for that one. Can you ask dispatch whether it's covered by another arrangement?
  7. Kai: Yes. I'll give them the cable description and explain that the printers match but the extra box doesn't have a reference here.
  8. Dana: Please keep the booked quantity as two printer cartons while they check. There aren't three printers.
  9. Kai: Exactly. Two printer cartons linked to R62, plus one separate carton you've described as cables.
  10. Dana: Does the extra box cancel the whole collection booking, or can the record still show which two match?
  11. Kai: The original two are still identifiable under R62. It's the additional cable carton that needs clarification.
  12. Dana: Good. I don't want each printer labelled an unmatched item because I've brought out an extra box.
  13. Kai: I won't do that. I'll keep the matches and the extra-item query separate. Dispatch hasn't authorized adding the cables yet.
  14. Dana: So any collection amendment is still pending. Asking you about the box hasn't changed the booking.
  15. Kai: Correct. It's a query, not an approved addition. It also isn't a permanent rejection of the cable carton.
  16. Dana: And no collected status yet. We're still checking the paperwork, not saying you've taken any of these cartons.
  17. Kai: Right. A reference match, loading, and a completed pickup are different stages. We've only clarified the identity question here.
  18. Dana: It isn't credit approval either, then. I'll avoid telling accounts that a return check means the credit has gone through.
  19. Kai: Yes. My report is R62, two identified printer cartons, one additional cable carton without a matching reference, query to dispatch.

Dialogue in the book, page 64 ↗
Three more conversations at work

Each short conversation has its own fictional situation and facts.

A third carton beside R62

A driver checks a return collection with a customer.

  1. Driver: Collection R62 lists two printer cartons. I can match these two to that reference.
  2. Customer: The third carton holds cables from the same purchase. I'd like those returned too.
  3. Driver: Do the cables have a separate return authorization or a matching collection reference?
  4. Customer: I only have the printer return message. I thought the purchase reference covered everything.
  5. Driver: A purchase reference and a return collection don't necessarily include the same items.
  6. Customer: Then please ask dispatch whether the cable carton can be added to today's collection.
  7. Driver: I can query it separately. Until confirmed, it remains an additional request, not part of R62.
  8. Customer: That makes sense. Please don't describe all three as printers in your collection record.
  9. Driver: I won't. The two printer cartons match; the cable carton still needs a booking decision.
  10. Customer: Thank you. I'll look for the cable authorization while dispatch checks that separate request.
Refusal because of a reported duplicate

A driver speaks with a receiver who declines a shipment.

  1. Driver: You're declining order seven sixty. Could you explain the reason for the delivery record?
  2. Receiver: Our office believes it's a duplicate. We received what looks like this order yesterday.
  3. Driver: Have you confirmed the two order references match, or is that still being checked?
  4. Receiver: It's still being checked. I can report our concern, but I can't prove duplication yet.
  5. Driver: I'll record refused delivery, with your reported reason, rather than confirmed duplicate order.
  6. Receiver: Please include that I declined it, not that nobody was here to receive it.
  7. Driver: Certainly. Customer service can compare the references and discuss the order with your buyer.
  8. Receiver: Can you promise that refusing this one will automatically cancel the invoice as well?
  9. Driver: I can't decide that. Delivery status and billing decisions need separate review by customer service.
  10. Receiver: Understood. Record today's refusal and the reason as reported, with the billing question outstanding.
The label points to an old return

A driver checks a collection label before matching it to the booking.

  1. Customer: Here's the return carton. I reused the box, so the label should already be there.
  2. Driver: The visible return label shows R19, but today's collection booking is for R91.
  3. Customer: Those numbers look similar. Is the label perhaps from my previous return last month?
  4. Driver: That could explain it, but we need to match this carton to today's actual authorization.
  5. Customer: My confirmation message says R91 for one lamp carton collected from this address.
  6. Driver: That matches the booking details. The old label still creates a destination-reference discrepancy.
  7. Customer: Please report that specifically, rather than saying I have no return approval at all.
  8. Driver: I will. You have an R91 confirmation; the carton displays a different reference.
  9. Customer: Who can tell us how the correct collection identification should be provided now?
  10. Driver: Dispatch's returns contact can resolve that label question before this carton is accepted for collection.

F Say it

Two scenarios to practice

Two booked cartons and one extra
  1. Read R62 with two printer cartons, then identify the additional cable carton.
  2. Repeat the distinction between two matched cartons and one unmatched carton.

On your own: read both roles. With a partner: swap roles after the first reading. Use the completed conversation to check any line you need to repeat.

Match another return accurately

Return S73 lists three monitor cartons. The customer presents those three and an extra keyboard carton without a matching reference. Dispatch can query the extra item; no amendment or collection is confirmed.

Choose one reply for each gap, then read the four turns in order. Check the explanation after each choice.

1. Driver: "S73 lists three ___ cartons."

Answer and explanation

C. monitor

Monitor cartons are the goods named in the recorded return scope.

2. Customer: "The extra carton contains a ___."

Answer and explanation

A. keyboard

The keyboard carton is the additional item lacking a matching reference.

3. Driver: "The extra carton remains ___."

Answer and explanation

A. unmatched

No record has yet linked the additional carton to the booking.

4. Customer: "The query does not mean collection is ___."

Answer and explanation

C. completed

Reference clarification does not establish that any pickup has occurred.

Read the completed exchange twice, switching roles. Keep the printed facts and professional responsibilities unchanged.

Lesson 07: Matching a return to its collection
Original fictional case: Driver Kai arrives for return collection R62, which lists two printer cartons. Customer Dana presents those two identifiable cartons and a third carton of cables without a matching collection reference. Dispatch can query the extra carton, but no authorization to add it has been received. The two printer cartons remain identifiable under R62. The conversation establishes the mismatch and next query; it does not confirm that any carton has been loaded, collected, rejected permanently, or credited to the customer.
Language workshop: Two booked cartons and one extra
Communication goal: Match goods to a return reference, isolate an unmatched item, and request clarification without silently expanding the collection.
Language guidance: R62 matches two printer cartons. Three cartons are presented, but the cable carton has no matching reference. The extra-item query should not change the original two-carton description or make the two identified cartons disappear from the record.
Useful expressions (preserve the supplied facts): I am here for return collection R62. / The booking lists two printer cartons. / These two printer cartons match R62. / The third carton contains cables according to your description. / I do not have a matching reference for that carton. / The original two are identifiable; the cable carton is the open question. / Is there a separate collection reference for the cables? / I cannot treat R62 as a three-carton booking without confirmation. / Dispatch can query the additional carton. / No collection amendment has been authorized yet. / Unmatched does not mean permanently rejected. / Two printer cartons, not three printer cartons. / Matching the reference does not mean the goods are already collected. / This check does not approve a customer credit. / Two booked, three presented, with one unmatched cable carton. / The next question is whether the cable carton has an authorized collection arrangement.
Vocabulary: return reference: Identifier connecting goods to a particular return arrangement. / collection booking: Recorded arrangement for picking up specified goods. / return authorization: Approval for specified goods to be returned under stated conditions. / return merchandise authorization: Formal return identifier or process, commonly RMA. / matching reference: Identifier consistent with the relevant record. / unmatched item: Item not linked to the stated collection record. / printer carton: Outer box identified as containing a printer in the return description. / cable carton: Outer box presented as containing cables. / additional carton: Box presented beyond the recorded collection quantity. / booked quantity: Number of units specified in the collection arrangement. / presented quantity: Number of units offered by the customer at collection. / collection scope: Items included in the specific pickup arrangement. / reference match: Confirmed correspondence between an item and a record identifier. / return label: Label linking a package to return-shipping information. / serial number: Identifier assigned to a particular individual product. / item description: Recorded wording identifying the type of goods. / packing description: Account of how goods are grouped or packaged. / collection amendment: Approved change to the pickup arrangement. / query: Request for clarification, not an approved change. / item separation: Keeping matched and unmatched items distinct in the discussion. / loaded status: Record that goods have been placed on the vehicle. / collected status: Record that a pickup has actually occurred. / credit approval: Authorized financial credit, separate from return collection. / return disposition: Decision about what happens to returned goods under the actual process.
Speaking task: Read R62 with two printer cartons, then identify the additional cable carton. Repeat the distinction between two matched cartons and one unmatched carton. Read the closing lines without claiming a pickup, booking amendment, or financial credit.
Partner: Kai: Driver / Dana: Customer
Guided response task: Select supplied answers for the dialogue gaps. Check the explanations, then read the completed exchange aloud; no original writing is required.
Optional second-round challenge: Return S73 lists three monitor cartons. The customer presents those three and an extra keyboard carton without a matching reference. Dispatch can query the extra item; no amendment or collection is confirmed.
Model for comparison AFTER my attempt, not a response to give me first: Kai: Right. A reference match, loading, and a completed pickup are different stages. We've only clarified the identity question here.
Dana: It isn't credit approval either, then. I'll avoid telling accounts that a return check means the credit has gone through.
Kai: Yes. My report is R62, two identified printer cartons, one additional cable carton without a matching reference, query to dispatch.
Dana: That's accurate. Keep the collection scope clear while you ask about the cables. Don't change the description to three printers.

Rehearse with an AI colleague — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Interactive workplace role-play
1. Use the supplied case or dialogue as the starting situation. Give a two-sentence briefing and offer two relevant professional roles for me to choose from. For a supplied dialogue, use its actual roles. Ask which role I want, then stop and wait. Do not write my replies.
2. After I choose, identify who you will play and the immediate communication goal. Play the other professional; where a meeting requires a third person, label each of your speakers clearly. Start with one natural workplace turn and wait for my reply. Keep most turns to one to three sentences and ask no more than one question at a time.
3. Let the exchange develop across six to ten learner turns, or end earlier if I type "feedback". Respond to what I actually say. Include a plausible clarification, disagreement or tradeoff without silently changing the starting facts. Mark any added constraint as a fictional second-round variation. Do not resolve approvals, evidence or commitments that remain uncertain.
4. Stay in role during the exchange. If my meaning is unclear, ask for clarification naturally. Give a hint only if I ask or cannot proceed. Do not deliver a model conversation in advance.
5. At the debrief, quote two of my phrases: one successful choice and one worth improving. Check factual accuracy, language, register and whether the next step was clear. Give up to three focused suggestions. Ask me to retry the weakest turn; wait. Only then offer an alternative wording and a harder replay.
Use voice replies when available. If I prefer selections, offer four plausible spoken replies and explain my choice before continuing. Do not require written compositions.

08Closing a route with a clear handoffOne missing upload, one refusal+

Your goal

Hand over distinct delivery outcomes and unfinished records, attributing a reported reason and assigning each follow-up explicitly.

This lesson in the book, page 67 ↗

A Read the situation

One missing upload, one refusal

Route 12 is ending. Driver Luis tells evening dispatcher Jo that stop 7 was received by Mei at 16:05, but the receipt has not uploaded. Stop 8 was refused because the customer reported a duplicate order; that reported reason has not been independently verified. Jo accepts responsibility for checking the stop-7 receipt upload and contacting customer service about stop 8. Neither follow-up is completed during this handover, and no refund, replacement delivery, or final route closure is established.

Luis: Driver / Jo: Evening dispatcher

Check the facts · 3 questions

Choose the answer supported by the briefing. Do not assume that a proposed action has already happened.

1. What happened at stop 7?

Answer and explanation

C. Mei received the shipment at 16:05, but the receipt upload is missing

The physical receipt occurred, while its electronic document upload remains unfinished.

2. What is the known reason given at stop 8?

Answer and explanation

C. The customer reported a duplicate order

Duplicate order is the customer's stated reason for refusal, not an independently verified finding.

3. What does Jo accept?

Answer and explanation

B. Both unfinished follow-ups

Jo takes ownership of the receipt check and customer-service contact without completing them.

B Find the words

Vocabulary and collocations

Stop 7 was received by Mei at 16:05; its receipt upload is missing. Stop 8 was refused with a customer-reported duplicate-order reason. These are different outcomes, not two undelivered shipments or two completed follow-ups.

24 field terms and natural word combinations
route handoff
Transfer of route information and unfinished responsibilities.give a route handoff
route closure
Completion of the required route-ending process.verify route closure
stop outcome
What actually happened at an individual delivery stop.state the stop outcome
received by
Phrase naming the person who received goods.record received by Mei
receipt timestamp
Time associated with the receiving event.preserve the receipt timestamp
delivery receipt
Record associated with the receiving event.locate the delivery receipt
proof of delivery
Evidence of delivery under the applicable process, often POD.check proof of delivery
receipt upload
Transfer of the receipt into the required electronic system.verify the receipt upload
upload pending
Status indicating the electronic transfer is unfinished.report upload pending
synchronization
Updating records between devices or systems.check synchronization status
record completeness
Whether all required information and records are present.verify record completeness
missing document
Required record not currently available where expected.follow up a missing document
refused delivery
Delivery the intended receiving party declines to accept.record a refused delivery
refusal reason
Explanation given for declining a delivery.attribute the refusal reason
reported duplicate
Alleged repetition of an order, not independently verified here.record the reported duplicate
customer service
Team handling customer queries and resolution processes.contact customer service
independent verification
Check separate from the original person's statement.request independent verification
open item
Task or question still needing action.list the open items
action owner
Person responsible for the next defined task.name the action owner
ownership acceptance
Agreement to take responsibility for follow-up.confirm ownership acceptance
completion status
Whether a task has actually been finished.preserve completion status
route summary
Concise account of outcomes across a delivery route.provide a route summary
read-back confirmation
Repetition that checks the accuracy of a handoff.request read-back confirmation
resolution
Established outcome addressing an open issue.distinguish referral from resolution

Jo accepting responsibility changes who owns the next actions, not whether the actions are finished. A reported duplicate is not an independently confirmed duplicate, and a missing upload is not proof that the physical receiving event never happened.

C Notice the language

Name the outcome, the unfinished record, and the owner

End-of-route summaries often compress different problems into everything is done or two failed deliveries. Separate the physical outcome from the record status at each stop. Attribute the stated reason for a refusal and confirm the next person owns the remaining work.

16 useful phrases, grouped by purpose
Name the route
I am handing over the open items from route 12.
Separate the stops
Stop 7 and stop 8 have different issues.
State the receiving event
Mei received stop 7 at 16:05.
State the missing record
The receipt has not uploaded.
Avoid a false delivery failure
The missing upload does not mean the goods were not received.
Give the second outcome
Stop 8 was refused.
Attribute the reason
The customer reported a duplicate order.
Preserve uncertainty
That reason has not been independently verified.
Assign the first action
Please check the stop-7 receipt upload.
Assign the second action
Please contact customer service about stop 8.
Accept ownership
I will take both follow-ups.
Avoid false completion
Taking ownership does not complete either check.
Ask for a read-back
Please repeat the two actions and their current status.
Keep remedies unconfirmed
No refund or replacement delivery is confirmed.
Preserve the timestamp
Keep 16:05 with the stop-7 receiving record.
Close the handoff
Jo owns both open items; their outcomes remain pending.
6 language and precision notes
Received versus uploaded
The physical event and electronic record transfer are separate facts.
Reported
Attributes the duplicate-order explanation to the customer.
Because
Can state the customer's reason for refusal without proving the underlying duplicate.
Will check versus checked
Future action must not be written as completed verification.
Both
Applies to the two distinct follow-ups, not two identical delivery outcomes.
Closed
Requires actual route-closeout conditions, not merely a handoff conversation.

D Check your understanding

Choose the precise message

Choose one best answer using the case facts and the stated purpose.

1. Which stop-7 statement is accurate?

Answer and explanation

A. Received by Mei at 16:05; receipt upload pending.

The statement preserves the receiver, timestamp, and separate unfinished electronic record.

2. Which stop-8 wording preserves attribution?

Answer and explanation

C. The customer reported a duplicate order and refused the delivery.

The customer's explanation must remain attributed rather than promoted to a verified cause.

3. Which handoff wrongly claims completion?

Answer and explanation

C. Jo accepted the tasks, so both issues are resolved.

Ownership acceptance assigns future work but does not supply its completed outcome.

4. Which action pair belongs to Jo?

Answer and explanation

B. Check stop-7 receipt upload and contact customer service about stop 8.

The two assigned tasks match the distinct unfinished items in the briefing.

Check explanations in the book, page 99 ↗

E Conversations

One missing upload, one refusal

Extended conversation · 20 turns · 10 gaps

Luis: Driver / Jo: Evening dispatcher

Complete the numbered gaps. Use each bank entry once, without changing its form.

Word bank
  • Independent verification
  • Route closure
  • stop outcome
  • receipt timestamp
  • receipt upload
  • Record completeness
  • customer service
  • read-back confirmation
  • refusal reason
  • action owner
  1. Luis
    Jo, I've got two open items from route twelve before I finish. Stop seven is a receipt issue; stop eight is a refusal.
  2. Jo
    Go ahead. Start with the 1 for stop 7, then tell me what remains unfinished. I do not want to merge it with stop 8.
  3. Luis
    Mei received stop seven at sixteen oh five. The receipt hasn't uploaded, so you won't find the completed electronic record yet.
  4. Jo
    I will check the 2. The receiving event is recorded as Mei at 16:05, and the missing upload is the follow-up issue.
  5. Luis
    Yes. Please don't mark that stop undelivered just because the upload is missing. The goods were received by Mei.
  6. Jo
    Understood. 3 is still in question, but the missing document does not erase your report that Mei received the shipment.
  7. Luis
    At stop eight, the customer refused it and said the order was a duplicate. I haven't independently checked whether that's true.
  8. Jo
    I will keep the 4 attributed to the customer. The wording should not become a finding that our company created a duplicate.
  9. Luis
    Please have customer service review that account. I didn't agree a refund or promise that we'd deliver it again.
  10. Jo
    I will contact 5 about stop 8. No refund, replacement delivery, or other resolution is confirmed by this handoff.
  11. Luis
    Can you take both jobs: checking the stop-seven upload and contacting customer service about the stop-eight refusal?
  12. Jo
    Yes, I am the 6 for both follow-ups. I have accepted the tasks; I have not yet checked the upload or made the customer-service contact.
  13. Luis
    Thanks. Keep sixteen oh five with Mei at stop seven. It isn't the refusal time for stop eight.
  14. Jo
    I will preserve that 7 exactly. We have no supplied time for the stop-8 refusal, so I will not copy 16:05 onto it.
  15. Luis
    Could you read the two notes back before I leave? I want the different outcomes and next actions kept together correctly.
  16. Jo
    Here is the 8: stop 7 received by Mei at 16:05, upload pending; stop 8 refused for a customer-reported duplicate order. I own both follow-ups.
  17. Luis
    That's it. The duplicate is what the customer reported, not a verified ordering error. Both follow-ups are still outstanding.
  18. Jo
    Agreed. 9 of the duplicate is still absent. The receipt check and customer-service contact remain pending.
  19. Luis
    Then the handover's clear. I won't call the whole route closed just because you've agreed to take the follow-ups.
  20. Jo
    Correct. 10 is not established here. We have transferred the two open items with their facts, uncertainties, and next owner intact.

Answers and explanations
  1. stop outcome Turn 2. Stop outcome describes what actually happened at each location, before discussing unfinished records or follow-up.
  2. receipt upload Turn 4. Receipt upload is the unfinished electronic transfer, not an uncompleted physical delivery at stop seven.
  3. Record completeness Turn 6. Record completeness concerns the missing receipt in the system and must not erase the actual receiving event.
  4. refusal reason Turn 8. Refusal reason is the customer's reported duplicate order, not an independently established ordering error.
  5. customer service Turn 10. Customer service is the team Jo will approach about stop eight; that contact has not yet resolved the issue.
  6. action owner Turn 12. Action owner names Jo as responsible for both next steps without implying either step is already complete.
  7. receipt timestamp Turn 14. Receipt timestamp belongs to Mei's receiving event at stop seven and must not be reassigned to the refusal.
  8. read-back confirmation Turn 16. Read-back confirmation checks the two outcomes, pending tasks, and ownership before the driver leaves.
  9. Independent verification Turn 18. Independent verification of the alleged duplicate has not occurred, so the customer's reason remains an attributed report.
  10. Route closure Turn 20. Route closure requires the relevant process to be completed; this handoff only transfers the two unfinished actions.
Read the complete conversation
  1. Luis: Jo, I've got two open items from route twelve before I finish. Stop seven is a receipt issue; stop eight is a refusal.
  2. Jo: Go ahead. Start with the stop outcome for stop 7, then tell me what remains unfinished. I do not want to merge it with stop 8.
  3. Luis: Mei received stop seven at sixteen oh five. The receipt hasn't uploaded, so you won't find the completed electronic record yet.
  4. Jo: I will check the receipt upload. The receiving event is recorded as Mei at 16:05, and the missing upload is the follow-up issue.
  5. Luis: Yes. Please don't mark that stop undelivered just because the upload is missing. The goods were received by Mei.
  6. Jo: Understood. Record completeness is still in question, but the missing document does not erase your report that Mei received the shipment.
  7. Luis: At stop eight, the customer refused it and said the order was a duplicate. I haven't independently checked whether that's true.
  8. Jo: I will keep the refusal reason attributed to the customer. The wording should not become a finding that our company created a duplicate.
  9. Luis: Please have customer service review that account. I didn't agree a refund or promise that we'd deliver it again.
  10. Jo: I will contact customer service about stop 8. No refund, replacement delivery, or other resolution is confirmed by this handoff.
  11. Luis: Can you take both jobs: checking the stop-seven upload and contacting customer service about the stop-eight refusal?
  12. Jo: Yes, I am the action owner for both follow-ups. I have accepted the tasks; I have not yet checked the upload or made the customer-service contact.
  13. Luis: Thanks. Keep sixteen oh five with Mei at stop seven. It isn't the refusal time for stop eight.
  14. Jo: I will preserve that receipt timestamp exactly. We have no supplied time for the stop-8 refusal, so I will not copy 16:05 onto it.
  15. Luis: Could you read the two notes back before I leave? I want the different outcomes and next actions kept together correctly.
  16. Jo: Here is the read-back confirmation: stop 7 received by Mei at 16:05, upload pending; stop 8 refused for a customer-reported duplicate order. I own both follow-ups.
  17. Luis: That's it. The duplicate is what the customer reported, not a verified ordering error. Both follow-ups are still outstanding.
  18. Jo: Agreed. Independent verification of the duplicate is still absent. The receipt check and customer-service contact remain pending.
  19. Luis: Then the handover's clear. I won't call the whole route closed just because you've agreed to take the follow-ups.
  20. Jo: Correct. Route closure is not established here. We have transferred the two open items with their facts, uncertainties, and next owner intact.
Dialogue in the book, page 73 ↗
Three more conversations at work

Each short conversation has its own fictional situation and facts.

Received goods, pending upload

A driver hands route 12 to evening dispatcher Jo.

  1. Driver: Jo, Mei received stop seven at sixteen oh five; the proof of delivery upload is pending.
  2. Dispatcher: I'll take that follow-up. What happened at stop eight, which also shows an exception?
  3. Driver: The customer refused it, saying the order was a duplicate. That reason isn't independently verified.
  4. Dispatcher: So seven concerns missing digital evidence; eight concerns a physical refusal with a reported reason.
  5. Driver: Exactly. Please don't arrange redelivery solely because the proof of delivery hasn't uploaded.
  6. Dispatcher: I'll check its record first and pass stop eight to customer service for review.
  7. Driver: Could you confirm you're taking both items after my route ends this evening?
  8. Dispatcher: Yes, I'm the follow-up owner for the receipt check and the customer-service handoff.
  9. Driver: I'll leave the recipient, time, and refusal details together in the dispatch log.
  10. Dispatcher: I have them. The log now distinguishes received pending upload from refused pending order review.
A corrected contact number for tomorrow

A driver hands a confirmed information change to the next dispatcher.

  1. Driver: Tomorrow's Lakeside stop has a corrected receiving number. The old number reaches the sales office.
  2. Dispatcher: Who confirmed the new number, and does the receiving location change as well?
  3. Driver: The named receiver confirmed it today. The address and entrance remain exactly the same.
  4. Dispatcher: I'll update the receiving contact field, not replace every contact number on the account.
  5. Driver: Good. Sales still uses the old number, so deleting it everywhere would create another problem.
  6. Dispatcher: Does the change apply to tomorrow's booking only, or to future receiving calls too?
  7. Driver: The receiver said future receiving calls. I haven't checked any separate after-hours arrangement.
  8. Dispatcher: I'll label the number for receiving and leave after-hours details unchanged pending confirmation.
  9. Driver: Please include that distinction in tomorrow's route handoff so the next driver uses the right field.
  10. Dispatcher: Done. The handoff identifies the new receiving number without implying the sales contact is invalid.
A return that has not reached the depot

A returning driver gives the desk a status update from a parked vehicle.

  1. Dispatcher: Your app says return initiated. Have the two refused cartons already reached our depot?
  2. Driver: No. They remain on my vehicle; initiated records the return instruction, not depot receipt.
  3. Dispatcher: Thanks. Customer service was about to tell the sender that the return was received.
  4. Driver: Please correct that to return in progress. Depot intake still needs to confirm the handover.
  5. Dispatcher: I'll change the message before it goes out. Which original shipment reference applies?
  6. Driver: Shipment five twelve, two cartons refused this afternoon because the recipient reported a wrong order.
  7. Dispatcher: I have that. Is wrong order verified, or should we keep it as the recipient's report?
  8. Driver: Keep it attributed to the recipient. Customer service has not completed its order comparison.
  9. Dispatcher: Then we'll await intake confirmation before reporting the cartons as received back at the depot.
  10. Driver: Exactly. That leaves both the physical return and the order query accurately described.

F Say it

Two scenarios to practice

One missing upload, one refusal
  1. Read stop 7 with Mei at 16:05 and the separate pending receipt upload.
  2. Read stop 8 with the customer's reported duplicate reason, without calling it verified.

On your own: read both roles. With a partner: swap roles after the first reading. Use the completed conversation to check any line you need to repeat.

Hand over another pair of stops

On route 15, stop 3 was received by Noor at 12:25, but its receipt upload is pending. Stop 4 was refused after a customer reported an unwanted order. Dispatcher Ana accepts both follow-ups; neither is complete.

Choose one reply for each gap, then read the four turns in order. Check the explanation after each choice.

1. Driver: "Stop 3 was received by ___ at 12:25."

Answer and explanation

B. Noor

Noor is the named receiver for the physically completed receiving event.

2. Dispatcher: "The receipt upload remains ___."

Answer and explanation

A. pending

The physical delivery does not establish completion of the electronic upload.

3. Driver: "The unwanted-order reason was ___ by the customer."

Answer and explanation

A. reported

Reported preserves attribution without claiming independent verification of the customer's explanation.

4. Dispatcher: "The next action owner is ___."

Answer and explanation

C. Ana

Ana accepts both unfinished follow-ups without claiming they are already completed.

Read the completed exchange twice, switching roles. Keep the printed facts and professional responsibilities unchanged.

Build vocabulary and collocations — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Retrieve occupational vocabulary
1. Select four terms from the reference. Explain each in plain English, give two natural collocations and quote or create a short workplace example. Label invented examples. Contrast each term with a plausible confusable term; acknowledge regional variation.
2. Present one role-labelled exchange with one missing word or phrase and four choices A-D. Exactly one choice must fit the stated facts and grammar. Stop and wait for my answer before revealing it.
3. Explain all four options, then invite me to read the completed exchange aloud. Offer a sentence frame for optional spoken practice, never an open-ended writing assignment.
4. Repeat for the other three terms, one question at a time. Vary the correct letter and the situation; preserve the reference facts. If a choice is ambiguous, replace the item.
5. Finish with two new four-choice collocation questions for later review, with a clearly separate answer key. Do not claim to schedule a reminder.

Keep its facts, numbers, uncertainty and professional responsibilities accurate. Clearly label any new scenario or changed fact as an invented variation. Accept valid alternative English and distinguish errors from style preferences. Do not claim to certify my language level. Lesson 08: Closing a route with a clear handoff
Original fictional case: Route 12 is ending. Driver Luis tells evening dispatcher Jo that stop 7 was received by Mei at 16:05, but the receipt has not uploaded. Stop 8 was refused because the customer reported a duplicate order; that reported reason has not been independently verified. Jo accepts responsibility for checking the stop-7 receipt upload and contacting customer service about stop 8. Neither follow-up is completed during this handover, and no refund, replacement delivery, or final route closure is established.
Language workshop: One missing upload, one refusal
Communication goal: Hand over distinct delivery outcomes and unfinished records, attributing a reported reason and assigning each follow-up explicitly.
Language guidance: Stop 7 was received by Mei at 16:05; its receipt upload is missing. Stop 8 was refused with a customer-reported duplicate-order reason. These are different outcomes, not two undelivered shipments or two completed follow-ups.
Useful expressions (preserve the supplied facts): I am handing over the open items from route 12. / Stop 7 and stop 8 have different issues. / Mei received stop 7 at 16:05. / The receipt has not uploaded. / The missing upload does not mean the goods were not received. / Stop 8 was refused. / The customer reported a duplicate order. / That reason has not been independently verified. / Please check the stop-7 receipt upload. / Please contact customer service about stop 8. / I will take both follow-ups. / Taking ownership does not complete either check. / Please repeat the two actions and their current status. / No refund or replacement delivery is confirmed. / Keep 16:05 with the stop-7 receiving record. / Jo owns both open items; their outcomes remain pending.
Vocabulary: route handoff: Transfer of route information and unfinished responsibilities. / route closure: Completion of the required route-ending process. / stop outcome: What actually happened at an individual delivery stop. / received by: Phrase naming the person who received goods. / receipt timestamp: Time associated with the receiving event. / delivery receipt: Record associated with the receiving event. / proof of delivery: Evidence of delivery under the applicable process, often POD. / receipt upload: Transfer of the receipt into the required electronic system. / upload pending: Status indicating the electronic transfer is unfinished. / synchronization: Updating records between devices or systems. / record completeness: Whether all required information and records are present. / missing document: Required record not currently available where expected. / refused delivery: Delivery the intended receiving party declines to accept. / refusal reason: Explanation given for declining a delivery. / reported duplicate: Alleged repetition of an order, not independently verified here. / customer service: Team handling customer queries and resolution processes. / independent verification: Check separate from the original person's statement. / open item: Task or question still needing action. / action owner: Person responsible for the next defined task. / ownership acceptance: Agreement to take responsibility for follow-up. / completion status: Whether a task has actually been finished. / route summary: Concise account of outcomes across a delivery route. / read-back confirmation: Repetition that checks the accuracy of a handoff. / resolution: Established outcome addressing an open issue.
Speaking task: Read stop 7 with Mei at 16:05 and the separate pending receipt upload. Read stop 8 with the customer's reported duplicate reason, without calling it verified. Repeat Jo's two follow-ups and leave both marked unfinished.
Partner: Luis: Driver / Jo: Evening dispatcher
Guided response task: Select supplied answers for the dialogue gaps. Check the explanations, then read the completed exchange aloud; no original writing is required.
Optional second-round challenge: On route 15, stop 3 was received by Noor at 12:25, but its receipt upload is pending. Stop 4 was refused after a customer reported an unwanted order. Dispatcher Ana accepts both follow-ups; neither is complete.
Model for comparison AFTER my attempt, not a response to give me first: Luis: That's it. The duplicate is what the customer reported, not a verified ordering error. Both follow-ups are still outstanding.
Jo: Agreed. Independent verification of the duplicate is still absent. The receipt check and customer-service contact remain pending.
Luis: Then the handover's clear. I won't call the whole route closed just because you've agreed to take the follow-ups.
Jo: Correct. Route closure is not established here. We have transferred the two open items with their facts, uncertainties, and next owner intact.
END REFERENCE

Rehearse with an AI colleague — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Interactive workplace role-play
1. Use the supplied case or dialogue as the starting situation. Give a two-sentence briefing and offer two relevant professional roles for me to choose from. For a supplied dialogue, use its actual roles. Ask which role I want, then stop and wait. Do not write my replies.
2. After I choose, identify who you will play and the immediate communication goal. Play the other professional; where a meeting requires a third person, label each of your speakers clearly. Start with one natural workplace turn and wait for my reply. Keep most turns to one to three sentences and ask no more than one question at a time.
3. Let the exchange develop across six to ten learner turns, or end earlier if I type "feedback". Respond to what I actually say. Include a plausible clarification, disagreement or tradeoff without silently changing the starting facts. Mark any added constraint as a fictional second-round variation. Do not resolve approvals, evidence or commitments that remain uncertain.
4. Stay in role during the exchange. If my meaning is unclear, ask for clarification naturally. Give a hint only if I ask or cannot proceed. Do not deliver a model conversation in advance.
5. At the debrief, quote two of my phrases: one successful choice and one worth improving. Check factual accuracy, language, register and whether the next step was clear. Give up to three focused suggestions. Ask me to retry the weakest turn; wait. Only then offer an alternative wording and a harder replay.
Use voice replies when available. If I prefer selections, offer four plausible spoken replies and explain my choice before continuing. Do not require written compositions.

Guided response task: Select supplied answers for the dialogue gaps. Check the explanations, then read the completed exchange aloud; no original writing is required.
Optional second-round challenge: On route 15, stop 3 was received by Noor at 12:25, but its receipt upload is pending. Stop 4 was refused after a customer reported an unwanted order. Dispatcher Ana accepts both follow-ups; neither is complete.
Model for comparison AFTER my attempt, not a response to give me first: Luis: That's it. The duplicate is what the customer reported, not a verified ordering error. Both follow-ups are still outstanding.
Jo: Agreed. Independent verification of the duplicate is still absent. The receipt check and customer-service contact remain pending.
Luis: Then the handover's clear. I won't call the whole route closed just because you've agreed to take the follow-ups.
Jo: Correct. Route closure is not established here. We have transferred the two open items with their facts, uncertainties, and next owner intact.
END REFERENCE

More situations, different skills

Three additional workplace scenarios

Whose nine thirty?

Read back an appointment with its date and time zone, then compare an arrival estimate on the same clock.

Fictional shipment T84: receiving confirms 13 October 2026 at 09:30 Central Daylight Time (CDT, UTC-5). Dispatch uses Eastern Daylight Time (EDT, UTC-4). The parked driver's current ETA is 10:15 EDT that day. The gate requires its actual check-in process; no early entry is promised. Priya and Joel clarify the shared record.

Priya: Driver / Joel: Dispatcher

Complete the numbered gaps. Use each bank entry once, without changing its form.

Word bank
  • CDT
  • 10:30
  • fifteen
  • UTC
  • check-in procedure
  • time zone
  1. Priya
    I'm parked and checking T84. The appointment says nine thirty, but my dispatch screen uses Eastern time. Which clock does the booking mean?
  2. Joel
    Receiving confirmed nine thirty 1, Central Daylight Time, on thirteen October. That is their local appointment time.
  3. Priya
    Their offset is UTC minus five, and our Eastern daylight setting is UTC minus four. So our clock is an hour ahead of theirs.
  4. Joel
    Correct. The same appointment is 2 on the Eastern daylight clock. Don't replace the label and keep nine thirty unchanged.
  5. Priya
    Then nine thirty Central and ten thirty Eastern are one appointment, not two slots an hour apart. We haven't asked to reschedule it.
  6. Joel
    Exactly. In 3, Coordinated Universal Time, it is fourteen thirty. Keep the date with each version.
  7. Priya
    My ETA currently reads ten fifteen Eastern daylight time on thirteen October. That should be nine fifteen on the receiving clock.
  8. Joel
    Yes. Your estimate is 4 minutes before the appointment, not forty-five minutes late.
  9. Priya
    I'd nearly compared ten fifteen on my screen directly with the unlabelled nine thirty in the note. That would have given the wrong impression.
  10. Joel
    The 5 is part of the time, not an optional extra. Read both the number and the clock label.
  11. Priya
    Will the gate take me at nine fifteen Central if that estimate holds, or does the appointment only settle the booked time?
  12. Joel
    The 6 still applies. We don't have a promise of early entry or a parking instruction from this time conversion.
  13. Priya
    All right. I won't treat fifteen minutes early as permission to enter or unload. I'll use the actual site directions.
  14. Joel
    And keep the ETA provisional. Converting it to another clock doesn't turn the estimate into a guaranteed arrival.
  15. Priya
    Could you keep Central as the receiving reference and put Eastern beside it? That would help when the desk calls us back.
  16. Joel
    Yes: thirteen October, zero nine thirty CDT, equivalent to ten thirty EDT. I'll retain the same shipment reference, T84.
  17. Priya
    Do we use those daylight offsets for every date? I've seen the same place described as Central standard time elsewhere.
  18. Joel
    No. Verify the location and date. Standard and daylight labels have different offsets; don't carry today's conversion into a different season without checking.
  19. Priya
    My read-back is T84, thirteen October, nine thirty CDT; ten thirty EDT; fourteen thirty UTC. ETA ten fifteen EDT is nine fifteen CDT.
  20. Joel
    That's consistent. One appointment, one provisional ETA, the same date, and no automatic early-access permission. Those are the details the next dispatcher needs.

Answers and explanations
  1. CDT Turn 2. The appointment is 09:30 Central Daylight Time, not 09:30 on the dispatcher's Eastern clock.
  2. 10:30 Turn 4. 09:30 at UTC-5 is the same instant as 10:30 at UTC-4, on the same date.
  3. UTC Turn 6. Coordinated Universal Time gives a common reference: both local appointment times convert to 14:30 UTC.
  4. fifteen Turn 8. 09:15 CDT is fifteen minutes before the 09:30 CDT appointment, after both times are put on the same clock.
  5. time zone Turn 10. The time-zone label identifies which local clock a number belongs to; omitting it creates an invalid comparison.
  6. check-in procedure Turn 12. A confirmed appointment does not grant early gate entry; the actual check-in procedure remains applicable.
Read the complete conversation
  1. Priya: I'm parked and checking T84. The appointment says nine thirty, but my dispatch screen uses Eastern time. Which clock does the booking mean?
  2. Joel: Receiving confirmed nine thirty CDT, Central Daylight Time, on thirteen October. That is their local appointment time.
  3. Priya: Their offset is UTC minus five, and our Eastern daylight setting is UTC minus four. So our clock is an hour ahead of theirs.
  4. Joel: Correct. The same appointment is 10:30 on the Eastern daylight clock. Don't replace the label and keep nine thirty unchanged.
  5. Priya: Then nine thirty Central and ten thirty Eastern are one appointment, not two slots an hour apart. We haven't asked to reschedule it.
  6. Joel: Exactly. In UTC, Coordinated Universal Time, it is fourteen thirty. Keep the date with each version.
  7. Priya: My ETA currently reads ten fifteen Eastern daylight time on thirteen October. That should be nine fifteen on the receiving clock.
  8. Joel: Yes. Your estimate is fifteen minutes before the appointment, not forty-five minutes late.
  9. Priya: I'd nearly compared ten fifteen on my screen directly with the unlabelled nine thirty in the note. That would have given the wrong impression.
  10. Joel: The time zone is part of the time, not an optional extra. Read both the number and the clock label.
  11. Priya: Will the gate take me at nine fifteen Central if that estimate holds, or does the appointment only settle the booked time?
  12. Joel: The check-in procedure still applies. We don't have a promise of early entry or a parking instruction from this time conversion.
  13. Priya: All right. I won't treat fifteen minutes early as permission to enter or unload. I'll use the actual site directions.
  14. Joel: And keep the ETA provisional. Converting it to another clock doesn't turn the estimate into a guaranteed arrival.
  15. Priya: Could you keep Central as the receiving reference and put Eastern beside it? That would help when the desk calls us back.
  16. Joel: Yes: thirteen October, zero nine thirty CDT, equivalent to ten thirty EDT. I'll retain the same shipment reference, T84.
  17. Priya: Do we use those daylight offsets for every date? I've seen the same place described as Central standard time elsewhere.
  18. Joel: No. Verify the location and date. Standard and daylight labels have different offsets; don't carry today's conversion into a different season without checking.
  19. Priya: My read-back is T84, thirteen October, nine thirty CDT; ten thirty EDT; fourteen thirty UTC. ETA ten fifteen EDT is nine fifteen CDT.
  20. Joel: That's consistent. One appointment, one provisional ETA, the same date, and no automatic early-access permission. Those are the details the next dispatcher needs.
Put the estimate on the receiving clock

On 14 October, receiving's appointment is 16:45 EDT (UTC-4). The driver's clock uses CDT (UTC-5), and the ETA is 15:30 CDT that same day. No early-entry agreement is supplied.

Choose one reply for each gap, then read the four turns in order. Check the explanation after each choice.

1. Driver: The appointment on my Central daylight clock is ___ .

Answer and explanation

C. 15:45

Eastern daylight time is one hour ahead of Central daylight time, so 16:45 EDT equals 15:45 CDT.

2. Dispatcher: The appointment in UTC is ___ .

Answer and explanation

D. 20:45

Adding four hours to 16:45 EDT gives 20:45 UTC on the same date.

3. Driver: My ETA is ___ minutes before the appointment.

Answer and explanation

B. fifteen

15:30 CDT is fifteen minutes earlier than the equivalent appointment at 15:45 CDT.

4. Dispatcher: Early entry remains ___ .

Answer and explanation

C. unconfirmed

A clock conversion and early ETA do not establish permission to enter the receiving site.

Check the answers, then read both exchanges aloud. Swap roles on the second reading.
